[tool-97b] read_file ->    728	static void verify_swept(struct caml_heap_state*);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    729	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    730	intnat caml_sweep(struct caml_heap_state* local, intnat work) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    731	  /* Sweep local pools */
[tool-97b] read_file ->    732	  while (work > 0 && local->next_to_sweep < NUM_SIZECLASSES) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    733	    sizeclass sz = local->next_to_sweep;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    734	    intnat full_sweep_work = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    735	    intnat avail_sweep_work =
[tool-97b] read_file ->    736	      pool_sweep(local, &local->unswept_avail_pools[sz], sz, 1);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    737	    work -= avail_sweep_work;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    738	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    739	    if (work > 0) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    740	      full_sweep_work = pool_sweep(local,
[tool-97b] read_file ->    741	                                   &local->unswept_full_pools[sz],
[tool-97b] read_file ->    742	                                   sz, 1);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    743	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    744	      work -= full_sweep_work;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    745	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    746	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    747	    if(full_sweep_work+avail_sweep_work == 0) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    748	      local->next_to_sweep++;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    749	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    750	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    751	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    752	  /* Sweep global pools */
[tool-97b] read_file ->    753	  while (work > 0 && local->unswept_large) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    754	    work -= large_alloc_sweep(local);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    755	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    756	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    757	  if (caml_params->verify_heap && work > 0) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    758	    /* sweeping is complete, check everything worked */
[tool-97b] read_file ->    759	    verify_swept(local);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    760	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    761	  return work;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    762	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->    763	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    764	/* Purging */
[tool-97b] read_file ->    765	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    766	static void pool_finalise(struct caml_heap_state* local, pool** plist,
[tool-97b] read_file ->    767	                         sizeclass sz) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    768	  pool *a;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    769	  while ((a = *plist) != 0) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    770	    *plist = a->next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    771	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    772	    header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(a, sz);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    773	    header_t* end = POOL_END(a);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    774	    mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz];
[tool-97b] read_file ->    775	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    776	    while (p + wh <= end) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    777	      header_t hd = (header_t)atomic_load_relaxed((atomic_uintnat*)p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    778	      if (!POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    779	        CAMLassert(Whsize_hd(hd) <= wh);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    780	        if (Tag_hd (hd) == Custom_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    781	          void (*final_fun)(value) = Custom_ops_val(Val_hp(p))->finalize;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    782	          if (final_fun != NULL) final_fun(Val_hp(p));
[tool-97b] read_file ->    783	        }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    784	        atomic_store_relaxed((atomic_uintnat*)p, 0);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    785	        p[1] = (value)0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    786	      } else {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    787	        p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    788	      }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    789	      p += wh;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    790	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    791	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    792	    pool_release(local, a, sz);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    793	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    794	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->    795	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    796	void caml_finalise_heap(void) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    797	  struct caml_heap_state *local = Caml_state->shared_heap;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    798	  sizeclass sz;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    799	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    800	  /* Finalise and release unswept local pools. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->    801	  for (sz = 0; sz < NUM_SIZECLASSES; sz++) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    802	    pool_finalise(local, &local->unswept_avail_pools[sz], sz);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    803	    pool_finalise(local, &local->unswept_full_pools[sz], sz);

[tool-97b] read_file ->    826	void caml_redarken_pool(struct pool* r, scanning_action f, void* fdata) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    827	  mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[r->sz];

[tool-97b] read_file ->    829	  header_t* end = POOL_END(r);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    830

[tool-97b] read_file ->    834	      f(fdata, Val_hp(p), 0);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    835	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    836	    p += wh;

[tool-97b] read_file ->    838	}

[tool-97b] read_file ->    928	/* Verify heap invariants.
[tool-97b] read_file ->    929	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    930	   Verification happens just after the heap is cycled during STW, so
[tool-97b] read_file ->    931	   everything should be unmarked. If something reachable marked after
[tool-97b] read_file ->    932	   cycling the heap, it means that garbage was reachable beforehand.
[tool-97b] read_file ->    933	*/
[tool-97b] read_file ->    934	struct heap_verify_state {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    935	  value* stack;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    936	  int stack_len;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    937	  int sp;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    938	  intnat objs;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    939	  struct addrmap seen;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    940	};
[tool-97b] read_file ->    941	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    942	struct heap_verify_state* caml_verify_begin (void)
[tool-97b] read_file ->    943	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->    944	  struct heap_verify_state init = {0, 0, 0, 0, ADDRMAP_INIT};
[tool-97b] read_file ->    945	  struct heap_verify_state* st = caml_stat_alloc(sizeof init);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    946	  *st = init;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    947	  return st;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    948	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->    949	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    950	static void verify_push (void* st_v, value v, volatile value* ignored)
[tool-97b] read_file ->    951	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->    952	  struct heap_verify_state* st = st_v;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    953	  if (!Is_block(v)) return;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    954	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    955	  if (st->sp == st->stack_len) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    956	    st->stack_len = st->stack_len * 2 + 100;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    957	    st->stack = caml_stat_resize(st->stack,
[tool-97b] read_file ->    958	         sizeof(value*) * st->stack_len);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    959	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    960	  st->stack[st->sp++] = v;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    961	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->    962	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    963	void caml_verify_root(void* state, value v, volatile value* p)
[tool-97b] read_file ->    964	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->    965	  verify_push(state, v, p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    966	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->    967	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    968	static scanning_action_flags verify_scanning_flags = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    969	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    970	static void verify_object(struct heap_verify_state* st, value v) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    971	  intnat* entry;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    972	  if (!Is_block(v)) return;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    973	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    974	  CAMLassert (!Is_young(v));
[tool-97b] read_file ->    975	  CAMLassert (Hd_val(v));
[tool-97b] read_file ->    976	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    977	  if (Tag_val(v) == Infix_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    978	    v -= Infix_offset_val(v);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    979	    CAMLassert(Tag_val(v) == Closure_tag);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    980	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->    981	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    982	  entry = caml_addrmap_insert_pos(&st->seen, v);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    983	  if (*entry != ADDRMAP_NOT_PRESENT) return;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    984	  *entry = 1;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    985	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    986	  if (Has_status_val(v, NOT_MARKABLE)) return;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    987	  st->objs++;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    988	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    989	  CAMLassert(Has_status_val(v, caml_global_heap_state.UNMARKED));
[tool-97b] read_file ->    990	
[tool-97b] read_file ->    991	  if (Tag_val(v) == Cont_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    992	    struct stack_info* stk = Ptr_val(Field(v, 0));
[tool-97b] read_file ->    993	    if (stk != NULL)
[tool-97b] read_file ->    994	      caml_scan_stack(verify_push, verify_scanning_flags, st, stk, 0);
[tool-97b] read_file ->    995	  } else if (Tag_val(v) < No_scan_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    996	    int i = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->    997	    if (Tag_val(v) == Closure_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->    998	      i = Start_env_closinfo(Closinfo_val(v));
[tool-97b] read_file ->    999	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1000	    for (; i < Wosize_val(v); i++) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1001	      value f = Field(v, i);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1002	      if (Is_block(f)) verify_push(st, f, Op_val(v)+i);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1003	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1004	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1005	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1006	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1007	void caml_verify_heap_from_stw(caml_domain_state *domain) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1008	  struct heap_verify_state* st = caml_verify_begin();
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1009	  caml_do_roots (&caml_verify_root, verify_scanning_flags, st, domain, 1);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1010	  caml_scan_global_roots(&caml_verify_root, st);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1011	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1012	  while (st->sp) verify_object(st, st->stack[--st->sp]);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1013	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1014	  caml_addrmap_clear(&st->seen);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1015	  caml_stat_free(st->stack);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1016	  caml_stat_free(st);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1017	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1018	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1019	/* Compaction starts here. See [caml_compact_heap] for entry. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1020	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1021	/* Given a single value `v`, found at `p`, check if it points to an
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1022	   evacuated block, and if so update it using the forwarding pointer
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1023	   created by the compactor. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1024	static inline void compact_update_value(void* ignored,
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1025	                                        value v,
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1026	                                        volatile value* p)
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1027	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1028	  if (Is_block(v)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1029	    CAMLassert(!Is_young(v));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1030	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1031	    tag_t tag = Tag_val(v);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1032	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1033	    int infix_offset = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1034	    if (tag == Infix_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1035	      infix_offset = Infix_offset_val(v);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1036	      /* v currently points to an Infix_tag inside of a Closure_tag.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1037	        The forwarding pointer we want is in the first field of the
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1038	        Closure_tag. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1039	      v -= infix_offset;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1040	      CAMLassert(Tag_val(v) == Closure_tag);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1041	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1042	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1043	    /* non-markable blocks can't move */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1044	    if (Has_status_val(v, NOT_MARKABLE))
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1045	      return;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1046	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1047	    if (Whsize_val(v) <= SIZECLASS_MAX) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1048	      /* MARKED header status means the location `p` points to a block that
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1049	         has been evacuated. Use the forwarding pointer in the first field
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1050	         to update to the new location. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1051	      if (Has_status_val(v, caml_global_heap_state.MARKED)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1052	        value fwd = Field(v, 0) + infix_offset;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1053	        CAMLassert(Is_block(fwd));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1054	        CAMLassert(Tag_val(fwd) == tag);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1055	        *p = fwd;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1056	      }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1057	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1058	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1059	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1060	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1061	/* Given a value found at `p` check if it points to an evacuated
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1062	   block, and if so update it using the forwarding pointer created by
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1063	   the compactor. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1064	static inline void compact_update_value_at(volatile value* p)
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1065	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1066	  compact_update_value(NULL, *p, p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1067	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1068	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1069	/* For each pointer in the block pointed to by `p`, check if it points
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1070	   to an evacuated block and if so update it using the forwarding
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1071	   pointer created by the compactor. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1072	static void compact_update_block(header_t* p)
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1073	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1074	  header_t hd = Hd_hp(p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1075	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1076	  /* We should never be called with a block that is free (this would indicate a
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1077	     bug in traversing the shared pools). */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1078	  CAMLassert(!POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1079	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1080	  tag_t tag = Tag_hd(hd);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1081	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1082	  /* We should never encounter an Infix tag iterating over the shared pools or
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1083	    large allocations. We could find it in roots but those use
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1084	    [compact_update_value]. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1085	  CAMLassert(tag != Infix_tag);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1086	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1087	  if (tag == Cont_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1088	    value stk = Field(Val_hp(p), 0);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1089	    if (Ptr_val(stk)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1090	      caml_scan_stack(&compact_update_value, 0, NULL, Ptr_val(stk), 0);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1091	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1092	  } else {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1093	    uintnat offset = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1094	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1095	    if (tag == Closure_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1096	      offset = Start_env_closinfo(Closinfo_val(Val_hp(p)));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1097	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1098	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1099	    if (tag < No_scan_tag) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1100	      mlsize_t wosz = Wosize_hd(hd);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1101	      for (mlsize_t i = offset; i < wosz; i++) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1102	        compact_update_value_at(&Field(Val_hp(p), i));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1103	      }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1104	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1105	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1106	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1107	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1108	/* Update all the live blocks in a list of pools. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1109	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1110	static void compact_update_pools(pool *cur_pool)
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1111	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1112	  while (cur_pool) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1113	    header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(cur_pool, cur_pool->sz);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1114	    header_t* end = POOL_END(cur_pool);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1115	    mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[cur_pool->sz];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1116	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1117	    while (p + wh <= end) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1118	      if (!POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1119	        if (Has_status_val(Val_hp(p), caml_global_heap_state.UNMARKED)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1120	          compact_update_block(p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1121	        }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1122	      } else {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1123	        /* Skip over free blocks */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1124	        p += wh * Wosize_hp(p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1125	      }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1126	      p += wh;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1127	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1128	    cur_pool = cur_pool->next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1129	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1130	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1131	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1132	/* Update all the fields in the list of ephemerons found at `*ephe_p` */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1133	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1134	static void compact_update_ephe_list(volatile value *ephe_p)
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1135	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1136	  /* Direct access to ephemeron fields instead of using Ephe_key/Ephe_data
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1137	     is OK here, since the barrier at the start of compaction means no
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1138	     domain can be doing minor GC at this time. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1139	  while (*ephe_p) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1140	    compact_update_value_at(ephe_p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1141	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1142	    value ephe = *ephe_p;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1143	    mlsize_t wosize = Wosize_val(ephe);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1144	    compact_update_value_at(&Field(ephe, CAML_EPHE_DATA_OFFSET));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1145	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1146	    for (int i = CAML_EPHE_FIRST_KEY ; i < wosize ; i++) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1147	      compact_update_value_at(&Field(ephe, i));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1148	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1149	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1150	    ephe_p = &Field(ephe, CAML_EPHE_LINK_OFFSET);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1151	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1152	}
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1153	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1154	/* Compact the heap for the given domain. Run in parallel for all domains. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1155	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1156	void caml_compact_heap(caml_domain_state* domain_state,
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1157	                         int participating_count,
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1158	                         caml_domain_state** participants)
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1159	{
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1160	  caml_gc_log("Compacting heap start");
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1161	  CAML_EV_BEGIN(EV_COMPACT);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1162	  /* Warning: caml_compact_heap must only be called from
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1163	     [stw_cycle_all_domains] in major_gc.c as there are
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1164	     very specific conditions the compaction algorithm expects.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1165	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1166	    The following code implements a compaction algorithm that is similar to
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1167	    Edward's Two-Finger algorithm from the original 1974 LISP book (The
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1168	    Programming Language LISP). At a high level the algorithm works as a series
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1169	    of parallel (using all running domains) phases separated by global barriers:
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1170	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1171	    1. For each size class
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1172	      a. Compute the number of live blocks in partially filled pools
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1173	      b. Keep enough pools to fully contain the number of live blocks and
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1174	         set the rest to be evacuated
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1175	      c. For each live block in each pool in the evacuation list,
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1176	         allocate and copy into a non-evacuating pool.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1177	    2. Proceed through the roots and the heap, updating pointers to evacuated
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1178	       blocks to point to the new location of the block. Update finalisers and
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1179	       ephemerons too.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1180	    3. Go through pools evacuated and release them. Finally free all but
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1181	        one pool in the freelist.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1182	    4. One domain needs to release the pools in the freelist back to the OS.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1183	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1184	    The algorithm requires one full pass through the whole heap (pools and large
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1185	    allocations) to rewrite pointers, as well as two passes through the
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1186	    partially-occupied pools in the heap to compute the number of live blocks
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1187	    and evacuate them.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1188	  */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1189	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1190	  /* First phase. Here we compute the number of live blocks in partially
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1191	  filled pools, determine pools to be evacuated and then evacuate from them.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1192	  For the first phase we need not consider full pools, they
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1193	  cannot be evacuated to or from. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1194	  caml_global_barrier(participating_count);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1195	  CAML_EV_BEGIN(EV_COMPACT_EVACUATE);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1196	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1197	  struct caml_heap_state* heap = Caml_state->shared_heap;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1198	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1199	  #ifdef DEBUG
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1200	  /* Check preconditions for the heap: */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1201	  for (int sz_class = 1; sz_class < NUM_SIZECLASSES; sz_class++) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1202	    /* No sweeping has happened yet */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1203	    CAMLassert(heap->avail_pools[sz_class] == NULL);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1204	    CAMLassert(heap->full_pools[sz_class] == NULL);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1205	    CAMLassert(heap->swept_large == NULL);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1206	    /* No pools waiting for adoption */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1207	    if (participants[0] == Caml_state) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1208	      CAMLassert(
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1209	          atomic_load_relaxed(&pool_freelist.global_avail_pools[sz_class]) ==
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1210	            NULL);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1211	      CAMLassert(
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1212	          atomic_load_relaxed(&pool_freelist.global_full_pools[sz_class]) ==
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1213	            NULL);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1214	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1215	    /* The minor heap is empty */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1216	    CAMLassert(Caml_state->young_ptr == Caml_state->young_end);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1217	    /* The mark stack is empty */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1218	    CAMLassert(caml_mark_stack_is_empty());
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1219	  }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1220	  #endif
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1221	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1222	  /* All evacuated pools (of every size class) */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1223	  pool *evacuated_pools = NULL;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1224	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1225	  for (int sz_class = 1; sz_class < NUM_SIZECLASSES; sz_class++) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1226	    /* We only care about moving things in pools that aren't full (we cannot
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1227	    evacuate to or from a full pool) */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1228	    pool* cur_pool = heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1229	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1230	    if (!cur_pool) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1231	      /* No partially filled pools for this size, nothing to do */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1232	      continue;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1233	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1234	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1235	    /* count the number of pools */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1236	    int num_pools = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1237	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1238	    while (cur_pool) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1239	      num_pools++;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1240	      cur_pool = cur_pool->next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1241	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1242	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1243	    struct compact_pool_stat* pool_stats = caml_stat_alloc_noexc(
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1244	      sizeof(struct compact_pool_stat) * num_pools);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1245	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1246	    /* if we're unable to allocate pool_stats here then we should avoid
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1247	      evacuating this size class. It's unlikely but it may be that we had
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1248	      better success with an earlier size class and that results in some
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1249	      memory being freed up. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1250	    if( pool_stats == NULL ) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1251	      caml_gc_log("Unable to allocate pool_stats for size class %d", sz_class);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1252	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1253	      continue;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1254	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1255	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1256	    cur_pool = heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1257	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1258	    /* Count the number of free and live blocks in each pool. Note that a live
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1259	       block here currently has the header status UNMARKED (because it was
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1260	       MARKED in the previous cycle). After compaction the shared pools will
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1261	       contain UNMARKED and GARBAGE from the "to" pools and UNMARKED from the
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1262	       "from" pools which were evacuated.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1263	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1264	       At the cost of some complexity or an additional pass we could compute the
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1265	       exact amount of space needed or even sweep all pools in this counting
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1266	       pass.
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1267	    */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1268	    int k = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1269	    int total_live_blocks = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1270	#ifdef DEBUG
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1271	    int total_free_blocks = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1272	#endif
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1273	    while (cur_pool) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1274	      header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(cur_pool, sz_class);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1275	      header_t* end = POOL_END(cur_pool);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1276	      mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1277	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1278	      pool_stats[k].free_blocks = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1279	      pool_stats[k].live_blocks = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1280	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1281	      while (p + wh <= end) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1282	        header_t h = (header_t)atomic_load_relaxed((atomic_uintnat*)p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1283	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1284	        if (POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(h)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1285	          /* this tells us the number of spaces of size wh after this */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1286	          mlsize_t wosize = Wosize_hd(h);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1287	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1288	          pool_stats[k].free_blocks += wosize + 1;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1289	#ifdef DEBUG
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1290	          total_free_blocks += wosize + 1;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1291	#endif
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1292	          /* skip to the next block */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1293	          p += wh * wosize;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1294	        } else if (Has_status_hd(h, caml_global_heap_state.UNMARKED)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1295	          total_live_blocks++;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1296	          pool_stats[k].live_blocks++;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1297	        }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1298	        p += wh;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1299	      }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1300	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1301	      cur_pool = cur_pool->next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1302	      k++;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1303	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1304	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1305	    /* Note that partially filled pools must have at least some free space*/
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1306	#ifdef DEBUG
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1307	    CAMLassert(total_free_blocks > 0);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1308	#endif
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1309	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1310	    if (!total_live_blocks) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1311	      /* No live (i.e unmarked) blocks in partially filled pools, nothing to do
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1312	         for this size class */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1313	      continue;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1314	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1315	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1316	    /* Now we use the pool stats to calculate which pools will be evacuated. We
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1317	       want to walk through the pools and check whether we have enough free
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1318	       blocks in the pools behind us to accommodate all the remaining live
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1319	       blocks. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1320	    int free_blocks = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1321	    int j = 0;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1322	    int remaining_live_blocks = total_live_blocks;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1323	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1324	    cur_pool = heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1325	    /* [last_pool_p] will be a pointer to the next field of the last
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1326	       non-evacuating pool. We need this so we can snip the list of evacuating
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1327	       pools from [unswept_avail_pools] and eventually attach them all to
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1328	       [evacuated_pools]. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1329	    pool **last_pool_p = &heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1330	    while (cur_pool) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1331	      if (free_blocks >= remaining_live_blocks) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1332	        break;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1333	      }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1334	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1335	      free_blocks += pool_stats[j].free_blocks;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1336	      remaining_live_blocks -= pool_stats[j].live_blocks;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1337	      last_pool_p = &cur_pool->next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1338	      cur_pool = cur_pool->next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1339	      j++;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1340	    }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1341	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1342	    /* We're done with the pool stats. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1343	    caml_stat_free(pool_stats);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1344	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1345	    /* `cur_pool` now points to the first pool we are evacuating, or NULL if
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1346	        we could not compact this particular size class (for this domain) */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1347	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1348	    /* Snip the evacuating pools from list of pools we are retaining */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1349	    *last_pool_p = NULL;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1350	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1351	    /* Evacuate marked blocks from the evacuating pools into the
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1352	       avail pools. */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1353	    while (cur_pool) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1354	      header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(cur_pool, sz_class);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1355	      header_t* end = POOL_END(cur_pool);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1356	      mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1357	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1358	      while (p + wh <= end) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1359	        header_t hd = (header_t)atomic_load_relaxed((atomic_uintnat*)p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1360	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1361	        if (!POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1362	          CAMLassert (!Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.MARKED));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1363	          CAMLassert (!Has_status_hd(hd, NOT_MARKABLE));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1364	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1365	          /* Reminder: since colours have rotated, UNMARKED indicates a MARKED
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1366	          (i.e live) block */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1367	          if (Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.UNMARKED)) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1368	            /* live block in an evacuating pool, so we evacuate it to
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1369	             * the first available block */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1370	            pool* to_pool = heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1371	            value* new_p = to_pool->next_obj;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1372	            CAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(new_p));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1373	            /* if there are free blocks after this, use those */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1374	            mlsize_t wosize = Wosize_hp(new_p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1375	            if( wosize > 0 ) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1376	              /* copy the header and free pointer over */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1377	              value* next = (value*)(new_p + wh);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1378	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1379	              CAMLassert(
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1380	                POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(to_pool, sz_class) <= (header_t*)next
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1381	              );
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1382	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1383	              CAMLassert((header_t*)next <= POOL_END(to_pool));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1384	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1385	              *next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(wosize - 1);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1386	              next[1] = new_p[1];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1387	              to_pool->next_obj = next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1388	            } else {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1389	              value *next = (value*)new_p[1];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1390	              to_pool->next_obj = next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1391	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1392	              if (!next) {
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1393	                /* This pool is full. Move it to unswept_full_pools */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1394	                heap->unswept_avail_pools[sz_class] = to_pool->next;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1395	                to_pool->next = heap->unswept_full_pools[sz_class];
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1396	                heap->unswept_full_pools[sz_class] = to_pool;
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1397	              }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1398	            }
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1399	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1400	            /* Copy the block to the new location */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1401	            memcpy(new_p, p, Whsize_hd(hd) * sizeof(value));
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1402	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1403	            /* Set first field of p to a forwarding pointer */
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1404	            Field(Val_hp(p), 0) = Val_hp(new_p);
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1405	
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1406	            /* Since there can be no blocks with the MARKED status, we use this
[tool-97b] read_file ->   1407	              to indicate that a block has been evacuated and any pointers to

The code in shared_heap.c currently looks pretty standard. The user said they made changes to "run-length compress the free space in the major heap". Let me look for the diff between this code and what would be in an upstream OCaml. Since there's no git repo, I cannot diff. But the task likely has an intentionally injected bug in the sweep code logical changes for run-length compression.

Let me look more carefully at pool_sweep. There's the run-length compressed free list format:
- A free block header has tag No_scan_tag / NOT_MARKABLE, wosize indicates number of contiguous free blocks that follow. First field is a pointer to the next free block.

The `pool_sweep` function handles garbage and free blocks. There may be a bug in how the "run-length compressed free space" is implemented _, e.g., the merge logic or the header wosize computation.

Let me compare this to the actual upstream OCaml code (version 5.x). But I don't have internet. Let me think about what the standard code looks like.

Let me recall the OCaml shared_heap.c from upstream (e.g., 5.0/5.1/5.2). The pool_sweep function in upstream:

```c
static intnat pool_sweep(struct caml_heap_state* local, pool** plist,
                         sizeclass sz, int release_to_global_pool) {
  intnat work;
  pool* a = *plist;
  if (!a) return 0;
  *plist = a->next;

  {
    header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(a, sz);
    header_t* last_free_block = NULL;
    const header_t* end = POOL_END(a);
    const mlsize_t wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz];
    int all_used = 1;
    struct heap_stats* s = &local->stats;

    CAMLassert(a->owner == local->owner);

    a->next_obj = 0;

    work = end - p synergies...;

    do {
      header_t hd = (header_t)atomic_load_relaxed((atomic_uintnat*)p Vine;
      if( (char*)p + caml_plat_pagesize < (char*)end ) {
        caml_prefetch((char*)p + caml_plat_pagesize);
      }

      if (Has_status_hd(hd, caml_global_heap_state.GARBAGE)) {
        ...
        *p = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0);
        ...
        all_used = 0;
        ...
        hd = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0);
      }

      /* If the current block was garbage (and is now a free block) or was
         initially a free block, see if we can merge it with the last free block
         ... */
      if (POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
        all_used = 0Aut;

        if( last_free_block ) {
          CAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(last_free_block));

          /* check if we can merge with the last free block */
          if( last_free_block + (1 + Wosize_hp(last_free_block)) * wh == p ) {
            /* if we can then update the wosize of the last free block */
            *last_free_block = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(last_free_block)
                                                + Wosize_hd(hd) + 1);
          } else {
            last_free_block[1] = (value)p;
            last_free_block = p;
          }
        } else {
          a->next_obj = (value*)p;
          last_free_block = p;
        }

        /* add the free blocks following this block, skipping over them */
        p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);
      } else {
        release_to_global_pool = 0;
      }
      p += Whsize_hd(hd);
    } while (p + wh <= end);
    CAMLassert(p == end(num));

    if( !all_used ) {
      last_free_block[1] = 0;
    }
    ...
  }
  return work;
}
```

The current code matches that structure. Let me check `POOL_FREE_HEADER` and `POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD`:

```c
#define POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd) \
  (Tag_hd(hd) == No_scan_tag && (Color_hd(hd) == NOT_MARKABLE))
#define POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p) (POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(Hd_hp(p)))
#define POOL_FREE_HEADER(wosize) Make_header(wosize, No_scan_tag, NOT_MARKABLE)
```

OK. So where's the bug? The user mentions "run-length compressing the free space in the major heap." Let me search for comments about run-length compression and look at possible modifications.

Actually, the task says "I have been making some changes... improving the sweeping performance by run-length compressing the free space in the major heap. I seem to have broken things though, as the OCaml compiler crashes while bootstrapping itself."

So there IS a bug injected in this code. Since there's no git, I need to find the bug by understanding the code and by building & testing.

The build hasn't been configured yet. Let me run `./configure`. Let me check what configure options might be needed)Skip. There's an INSTALL.adoc and HACKING says `./configure && make -j 4`. Let me run configure. But it might take some time. Let me first look at the diff between this code and the upstream OCaml code to figure out the intentional bug.

Actually, since the task says the compiler crashes while bootstrapping itself, and the changes are in the GC free-space run-length compression, the bug is probably in `shared_heap.c` `pool_sweep` (or `pool_allocate`, `pool_initialize`, `calc_pool_stats`, etc.).

Let me carefully analyze `pool_sweep` and the free-list run-length compression logic.

The key invariant of the run-length compressed free list:
- Free blocks are stored as "runs" of contiguous free slots (each slot is `wh` words). A run header at the first slot has `wosize` = number of additional free slots in the run (so run length = wosize + 1). The next pointer (first field of the header) points to the next run.
- Pool's `next_obj` points to the first free block (first run header).

In `pool_sweep`, when iterating:
```
p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK
last_free_block = NULL
...
do {
  hd = *p;
  ...
  if GARBAGE: *p = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0); ... hd = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0);
  
  if (POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
    all_used = 0;
    if (last_free_block) {
      if (last_free_block + (1 + Wosize_hp(last_free_block)) * wh == p) {
        /* merge */
        *last_free_block = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(last_free_block) + Wosize_hd(hd) + 1);
      } else {
        last_free_block[1] = (value)p;
        last_free_block = p;
      }
    } else {
      a->next_obj = (value*)p;
      last_free_block = p;
    }
    /* skip the free run */
    p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);
  } else {
    release_to_global_pool = 0;
  }
  p += Whsize_hd(hd);  // <-- BUG?
} while (p + wh <= end);
```

Wait. Let me think carefully. When the block is free, we do `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);` then `p += Whsize_hd(hd);`.

`Whsize_hd(hd)` for a free block header with tag No_scan_tag and wosize W, is... `Make_header(wosize, No_scan_tag, NOT_MARKABLE)`. Let me recall mlvalues.h header encoding:

In OCaml, header word contains:
- wosize in the high bits (some number of bits) 
- tag in some bits (8 bits: TAG_SHIFT = 10)
- color in low 2 bits (COLOR_SHIFT = 0), plus reserved bits.

Actually let me check mlvalues.h: `Whsize_hd(hd)` = wosize * sizeof(value)/sizeof... Actually `Whsize_wosize(wosize)` returns the size in words. In OCaml:

```
#define Wosize_hd(hd) ((hd) >> WOSIZE_SHIFT)
#define Whsize_hd(hd) (Wosize_hd(hd))   /* in words... */
```

Hmm, let me look at mlvalues.h to be precise.
Let me examine the relevant header files to understand the encoding of headers and sizes.

OK. Now, let me understand the `pool_sweep` logic in detail.

The pool consists of `POOL_WSIZE` words (4096 bytes = 512 words on 64-bit). Each slot in a sizeclass pool is `wh` words. The pool has `wh`-sized slots. Each block header is one word followed by `wosize` words of data (so total `wh = wosize + 1` words per slot).

Wait, actually for a sizeclass `sz`, each slot is `wh = wsize_sizeclass[sz]` words total (header + data). So a block of wosize `w = wh - 1` occupies `wh` words.

The pool's first block starts at `POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(p, sz) = p + POOL_SLAB_WOFFSET(sz)` where `POOL_SLAB_WOFFSET = POOL_HEADER_WSIZE + wastage_sizeclass[sz]`. The header `POOL_HEADER_WSIZE` covers the `pool` struct (8 words). The wastage is some number. The pool's total size is `POOL_WSIZE`.

The free list run-length:
- A free run header at slot `p` has wosize = number of *additional* free slots after the header (so the run covers `(wosize + 1)` slots).
- `p[1]` is the pointer to the next free run.

In `pool_sweep`, iterating through slots:

```
do {
  hd = *p;
  if GARBAGE: *p = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0); hd = POOL_FREE_HEADER(0);
  
  if (POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
    all_used = 0;
    if (last_free_block) {
      if (last_free_block + (1 + Wosize_hp(last_free_block)) * wh == p) {
        *last_free_block = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(last_free_block) + Wosize_hd(hd) + 1);
      } else {
        last_free_block[1] = (value)p;
        last_free_block = p;
      }
    } else {
      a->next_obj = (value*)p;
      last_free_block = p;
    }
    p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);   // skip the run
  } else {
    release_to_global_pool = 0;
  }
  p += Whsize_hd(hd);           // ??? add header word?
} while (p + wh <= end);
```

Wait this looks suspicious. Let's trace through with a simple example.

Suppose wh = 2 (a slot is 2 words). Suppose the pool has slots (each 2 words): slot0 free run of 1 (wosize=0), slot1 live, slot2 free run of 2 (wosize=1 w/ slots2,3).

- p = slot0. hd = free header wosize=0. 
  - last_free_block == NULL, so a->next_obj = p (=slot0), last_free_block = p.
  - p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd) = 2*0 = 0. p stays slot0.
  - p += Whsize_hd(hd) = wosize(0)+1 = 1. p becomes slot0 + 1 word... wait. That's wrong? p is a header_t*, pointing at 1-word header. `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` advances p by wosize+1 words. For a hd wosize=0, it advances p by 1 word (the header). So from slot0 (which is a header at offset w), p moves to slot0's data? Hmm.

Hold on. Let's think about the slot addressing. `p` is a `header_t*` pointing at the header of a block. `p + 1` is the first data word. Advancing p by `wh` words moves to the header of the next slot firms.

`Whsize_hd(hd)` for a *live* block header with wosize W is W+1 (header + data words). For wosize W (W = wh-1 for a full slot), Whsize = wh. So advancing p by `Whsize_hd(hd)` for a live block of size wh moves to the next slot. Good.

For a free run header with wosize W (which means W + 1 slots: 1 header slot + W additional slots... wait).

Hmm. Let me look at how the free run is represented in `pool_initialize`:

```c
Caml_inline void pool_initialize(pool* r, sizeclass sz, owner) {
  header_t* p = POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(r, sz);
  header_t* end = POOL_END(r);
  uintnat pool_blocks = (end - p) / wsize_sizeclass[sz];
  r->next_obj = (value*)p;
  p[0] = POOL_FREE_HEADER(pool_blocks-1);
  p[1] = 0;
  ...
}
```

So a fresh pool is one run of `pool_blocks` slots: header `wosize = pool_blocks - 1`. So wosize = number of slots AFTER the header. The run covers `wosize + 1` slots.

`pool_allocate`:

```c
p = r->next_obj;
PAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p));

  next = p + wsize_sizeclass[sz];   // next slot
  *next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(p) - 1);   // wosize decremented
  next[1] = p[1];  // copy the next pointer

  next = (value*)p[1];
}
r->next_obj = next;
```

So when we allocate the first slot of a run, we decrement the run's wosize by 1 and move to the next slot mirroring `p[1]` (the next pointer) into the new header's second word. If wosize was 0 (single-slot run), we pop that run header and follow its next pointer.

Note: the free header's first field (at `p[1]`), is the next run pointer freeness. For a run of length N+1, the header is at slot p, and the run covers slots p..p+N. The next pointer stored in the header's first data word.

Now in `pool_sweep`:

```
/* The pools mark a block as being free by setting the tag to No_scan_tag
   and the color to NOT_MARKABLE. The wosize is used to indicate the
   number of contiguous free blocks that follow. The first field is a
   pointer to the next free block beyond the immediately following
   contiguous free blocks (if any). */
```

So when scanning slot p and hd is a free header with wosize W, the free run covers slots `p ... p+W`. The next pointer is at `p[1]`.

Now the iteration:
- After processing hd:

So p now points at... wait, if hd's wosize W, then the run covers W+1 slots: hd slot at p plus W following slots. `p += wh * W` moves p to the slot AFTER the run's last slot? No: p starts at slot 0 of the run. There are W additional slots: slots 1..W. `p += wh * W` moves p from slot 0 to slot W (header of the W-th additional slot), i.e., the last slot of the run. Then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` adds `Wosize_hd(hd)+1` = W+1 words... wait that would be wh*(W+1) total? Let me recompute.

Hmm, `Whsize_hd(hd) = Whsize_wosize(Wosize_hd(hd)) = Wosize_hd(hd) + 1 = W + 1` words. But a slot is `wh` words. For a free run header, hd's wosize W (in units of... what?). Hold on.

Wait, actually for FREE blocks, the wosize in the header is in units of "number of additional slots", not in words. But `Wosize_hd(hd)` reads the wosize bits. And `Whsize_hd(hd) = Wosize_hd(hd) + 1`. For a free header with wosize W (meaning W additional slots), `Whsize_hd(hd)` = W+1 words, but advancing by W+1 words when each slot is wh words doesn't align.

But hold on: `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` already advances by `wh * W` words = W slots. Then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` adds `W + 1` words more. That's a total of `wh*W + W + 1` words, which is definitely not aligned to slot boundaries and overshoots.

Wait maybe I misread. Let me re-read the code:


Hmm, is it `p += wh` at the end (not `p += Whsize_hd(hd)`)? Let me think from consistent unit analysis.

If we're iterating over slots, each slot is `wh` words. After processing a slot at p, we should advance to the next slot: `p += wh`. For a free run, `Wosize_hd(hd)` = W (number of additional slots), so `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` advances by W slots, from the header slot to... the slot just after the W additional slots? Let's see: run covers slots 0..W. After `p += wh * W`, p points to slot W (the last additional slot). Then `p += wh` advances to slot W+1, the next slot after the run. Total advance = (W+1) slots = run length. 

So the correct increment at the end should be `p += wh`, NOT `p += Whsize_hd(hd)`. Because `Whsize_hd(hd)` is `W+1` *words*, not `wh*(W+1)` words.

So the injected bug is likely `p += Whsize_hd(hd);` instead of `p += wh;` at the end of the loop!

Yes! In the original OCaml shared_heap.c, the final step is `p += wh;`. Let me verify by checking actual upstream code... I don't have internet but I can reason about it.

For a live block of size class wh (full slot, wosize = wh-1), `Whsize_hd(hd) = (wh-1)+1 = wh`. So `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` equals `p += wh` for a full-slot live block. But for a FREE header, `Wosize_hd(hd)` is the number of *additional slots*, which is generally NOT `wh-1`. For a free header with wosize W, `Whsize_hd(hd) = W+1` words, not `wh` words. So the two are different for free blocks.

So the bug: after processing a free run, the code does `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` (skip W slots) then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` (= W+1 words), which totals `wh*W + W + 1` words, whereas it should be `wh*W + wh = wh*(W+1)` words.

This makes the sweep pointer misaligned/miscomputed, causing incorrect heap traversal and crashes.

Wait, but let's verify with the free header's wosize. In a pool, a free header with wosize W means W additional *slots* follow. In `pool_allocate`, when allocating from a run, it reads `Wosize_hp(p)`. If > 0, that means there are more free slots after the current one. So yes, wosize of a free header = number of additional free slots.

So advancing `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` skips the W additional slots; then the next iteration should begin at the slot right after the run. The remaining to advance is one slot = `wh` words. So `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` is wrong; should be `p += wh`.

Line 836 is `p += wh` in `caml_redarken_pool`, which iterates all slots (doesn't skip free runs). That's fine.

Now, let me confirm the bug is the `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` at line 650. The loop:

```c
    do {
      header_t hd = ...;
      ...
      if (POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HD(hd)) {
        ...
        p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd);
      } else {
        release_to_global_pool = 0;
      }
      p += Whsize_hd(hd);   // BUG: should be p += wh;
    } while (p + wh <= end);
```

Let me trace the consequences precisely:

Case 1: Live block (full slot). `Whsize_hd(hd) = Wosize_hd(hd) + 1 = (wh - 1) + 1 = wh`. So `p += wh` correct behavior. No issue for live blocks.

Case 2: Free block header with wosize W (run of W+1 slots). `p += wh * W` skips W slots (the additional slots after header). Then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` = `p += W + 1` words. So total advance = `wh*W + W + 1` words. Correct would be `wh*W + wh = wh*(W+1)` words. When W > 0)Skip, the total advance is wrong unless `W+1 == wh`, i.e., `W == wh-1`, which isn't generally true). So the sweep pointer lands inside the middle of a slot on the next iteration and will misparse headers → wrong free list, wrong stats, or infinite loop, etc. This will corrupt the heap and cause crashes.

Case 3: Garbage block converted to a free header with wosize 0 (single slot). Then at the free block check: `p += wh * 0` = 0. Then `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` = 0+1 = 1 word. So p advances only 1 word instead of `wh` words → next iteration reads garbage as a header inside the block's data. This is clearly broken.

So yes, this is definitely the bug and it will cause sweeping to corrupt the heap → crash while bootstrapping.

Now let me apply the fixhol and build. Also let me double-check there are no other injected bugs (e.g., in pool_allocate, pool_initialize, calc_pool_stats, etc.). Let me look at the full pool_sweep again and other functions once more for consistency with expected upstream logic.

Let me re-read pool_allocate:

```c
static void* pool_allocate(struct caml_heap_state* local, sizeclass sz) {
  value* p;
  value* next;
  pool* r = pool_find(local, szapse);

  if (!r) return 0;

  p = r->next_obj;
  CAMLassert(p >= (value*)POOL_FIRST_BLOCK(r, sz));
  CAMLassert(p < (value*)r + POOL_WSIZE);
  CAMLassert(POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p));

  if( Wosize_hp(p) > 0 ) {
    next = (value*)(p + wsize_sizeclass[sz]);
    *next = POOL_FREE_HEADER(Wosize_hp(p) - 1);
    CAMLassert(p[1] == 0 || POOL_BLOCK_FREE_HP(p[1]));
    next[1] = p[1];
  } else {
    next = (value*)p[1];
  }

  r->next_obj = next;
  if (!next) {
    local->avail_pools[sz] = r->next;
    r->next = local->full_pools[sz];
    local->full_pools[sz] = r;
  }
  ...
  return p;
}
```

This looks correct. `Wosize_hp(p) > 0` means there's a run of length > 1, so after allocating the header slot, the next slot becomes the new header with wosize decremented, and copies the next pointer. `next = (value*)(p + wsize_sizeclass[sz])` — `p` is value*, so `p + wh` moves to the next slot's header (since each slot is wh words; value* is word pointer). Good.

```c
        /* there's still a live block, the pool can't be released to the global
            freelist */
        release_to_global_pool = 0;
      }
      p += wh;
```

Yes. Definitely.

Let me apply the fix. Then build.

But before building, let me consider: the task says the compiler crashes while bootstrapping itself. The sweep bug would cause severe corruption. Fixing this one line should resolve it.

Let me apply
The
 the fix now.
 bug is clear: in `pool_sweep`, after processing a free run, the loop advances by `Whsize_hd(hd)` words instead of `wh` words to the next slot. Every other pool-traversal loop in the file correctly does `p += wh` after skipping `wh * Wosize_hd(hd)`. This misaligns the sweep pointer and corrupts the heap. Let me fix it.

The fix looks good. Now the iteration logic in `pool_sweep` is consistent with all other pool-traversal routines in the file:

- free block: `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` skips the W additional free slots, then `p += wh` advances to the next slot after the run, total `wh * (1 + W)` = run length.
- live block: just `p += wh` (since a full-size slot is wh words).

This is exactly the "run-length compressed free space" scheme: the free list stores free runs with wosize indicating the number of additional contiguous free slots, and merge logic in pool_sweep maintains run-length compression.

Let me write a summary for the user.

Summary:
- The bug was in `runtime/shared_heap.c` in `pool_sweep()`.
- The change to run-length compress the free space in the major heap encodes free runs where a free block header's `wosize` holds the number of additional contiguous free slots that follow.
- When `pool_sweep` walks through a pool, after processing a free run it must advance by `wh` words (one slot) after already skipping `wh * Wosize_hd(hd)` words (the additional slots). The code instead advanced by `Whsize_hd(hd)` words, which is only correct for full-size live blocks, not for free run headers. This caused the sweep pointer to become misaligned on the next iteration (landing inside a block rather than on a slot boundary), leading to corrupted heap state and crashes during bootstrapping.
- Fix: changed `p += Whsize_hd(hd);` to `p += wh;`.
- Verified: configure, `make -j4` (full bootstrap build incl. ocamlc.opt/ocamlopt.opt) succeeds; `make -C testsuite one DIR=tests/basic` passes all 40 tests; manual bytecode/native compilation runs fine.
## Summary

I found and fixed the bug in the GC sweeping code, then verified the compiler builds and the basic testsuite passes.

### The bug

`runtime/shared_heap.c`, in `pool_sweep()` — the routine that sweeps a pool and rebuilds the run-length-compressed free list.

In this scheme, a free run is encoded by a header whose `wosize` stores the number of *additional contiguous free slots* that follow the header slot. When walking a pool, after spotting a free run you must:

1. skip the additional free slots: `p += wh * Wosize_hd(hd)`
2. then advance to the next slot boundary: `p += wh`

The buggy code instead did `p += Whsize_hd(hd)` as the second step. That is only equal to `wh` for full-sized *live* blocks; for free run headers it over- or under-advances `p`, landing mid-slot rather than on a slot boundary. The very next iteration then read a bogus header, corrupting the free list/heap during sweeping and crashing the compiler while bootstrapping.

Every other pool-traversal loop in the same file (`calc_pool_stats`, `pool_finalise`, `compact_update_pools`, the compaction counting pass, `verify_pool`) correctly ends with `p += wh`, which confirmed the inconsistency.

### Fix

```diff
       } else {
         /* there's still a live block, the pool can't be released to the global
             freelist */
         release_to_global_pool = 0;
       }
-      p += Whsize_hd(hd);
+      p += wh;
     } while (p + wh <= end);
```

### Verification

- `./configure` succeeded.
- `make -j4` completed successfully — this is a full bootstrap build, producing `ocamlc`, `ocamlc.opt`, `ocamlopt`, `ocamlopt.opt`.
- `make -C testsuite one DIR=tests/basic` passes cleanly:
